##### Raw second transcription

```yaml
```yaml
lines:
  - id: household.ctc_disallowed_claim
    printed: "(helper)"
    label: "Form 8862, who must file. You must file Form 8862 to claim the child tax credit or credit for other dependents if your child tax credit (refundable or nonrefundable depending on the tax year), additional child tax credit, or credit for other dependents for a year after 2015 was denied or reduced for any reason other than a math or clerical error. ... you won't be allowed to take either credit or the additional child tax credit for 2 years ... for 10 years."
    type: Bool
    expr: {op: copy, from: household.credit_disallowed}
    note: "The IRS text describes two situations for the CTC, ACTC and ODC. In the first, the credit for a year after 2015 was denied or reduced for a reason other than a math or clerical error, so Form 8862 is required. In the second, the filer is in a 2-year or 10-year ban for a reckless, intentional or fraudulent claim. The supplied interface has one combined question, household.credit_disallowed. It asks about these situations for the EIC (after 1996) and for the CTC/ACTC/ODC (after 2015) together, so the two credit families cannot be told apart. This line therefore copies that combined answer. As a result, a disallowance of the EIC alone also flags the CTC/ODC claim. The interface also has no input for the Form 8862 exceptions, such as filing Form 8862 for 2024 with the credit allowed for that year, or the other exceptions in the Form 8862 instructions. Those exceptions are not modeled, and a yes answer is treated as a disallowed or Form 8862 claim."
  - id: household.eic_disallowed_claim
    printed: "(helper)"
    label: "Form 8862, who must file. You must file Form 8862 if your EIC for a year after 1996 was reduced or disallowed for any reason other than a math or clerical error. ... Also, don't file Form 8862 or take the credit for the: 2 years after the most recent tax year for which there was a final determination that your EIC claim was due to reckless or intentional disregard of the EIC rules, or 10 years after the most recent tax year for which there was a final determination that your EIC claim was due to fraud."
    type: Bool
    expr: {op: copy, from: household.credit_disallowed}
    note: "The IRS text covers two situations for the EIC. In the first, the EIC for a year after 1996 was reduced or disallowed for a reason other than a math or clerical error, so Form 8862 is required. In the second, the filer is in a 2-year or 10-year ban. The only supplied input, household.credit_disallowed, combines the EIC history with the CTC/ACTC/ODC history in one question, so this line copies it. As a result, a CTC/ODC-only disallowance also flags the EIC claim. The interface has no input for the stated exceptions to filing Form 8862, and they are not modeled. The first exception is a prior Form 8862 for which the EIC was allowed and not reduced again. The second is taking the EIC without a qualifying child when the only earlier reason was a child found not to be a qualifying child."
```

Second:

```json
{"input": "household.credit_disallowed"}
```

Second transcription's note: The IRS text describes two situations for the CTC, ACTC and ODC. In the first, the credit for a year after 2015 was denied or reduced for a reason other than a math or clerical error, so Form 8862 is required. In the second, the filer is in a 2-year or 10-year ban for a reckless, intentional or fraudulent claim. The supplied interface has one combined question, household.credit_disallowed. It asks about these situations for the EIC (after 1996) and for the CTC/ACTC/ODC (after 2015) together, so the two credit families cannot be told apart. This line therefore copies that combined answer. As a result, a disallowance of the EIC alone also flags the CTC/ODC claim. The interface also has no input for the Form 8862 exceptions, such as filing Form 8862 for 2024 with the credit allowed for that year, or the other exceptions in the Form 8862 instructions. Those exceptions are not modeled, and a yes answer is treated as a disallowed or Form 8862 claim.

Ruling: open
Reviewed in: pass 4961a7f51d6c

<!-- part section household.eic_disallowed_claim -->

```

Second:

```json
{"input": "household.credit_disallowed"}
```

Second transcription's note: The IRS text covers two situations for the EIC. In the first, the EIC for a year after 1996 was reduced or disallowed for a reason other than a math or clerical error, so Form 8862 is required. In the second, the filer is in a 2-year or 10-year ban. The only supplied input, household.credit_disallowed, combines the EIC history with the CTC/ACTC/ODC history in one question, so this line copies it. As a result, a CTC/ODC-only disallowance also flags the EIC claim. The interface has no input for the stated exceptions to filing Form 8862, and they are not modeled. The first exception is a prior Form 8862 for which the EIC was allowed and not reduced again. The second is taking the EIC without a qualifying child when the only earlier reason was a child found not to be a qualifying child.

Ruling: open
Reviewed in: pass 4961a7f51d6c
